## 1.    Introduction/Context

South East Technological University (SETU) is committed to the highest standards of teaching and research,  and  professional  alignment  of  its  programmes  within  all  disciplines.  The  Master  of Pharmacy (MPharm) programme at SETU and the Department of Pharmacy upholds this commitment through its policies and procedures, and ensuring compliance with the accreditation requirements of the Pharmaceutical Society of Ireland (PSI). SETU and the Department of Pharmacy recognises the importance of, and its obligations with respect to Open Disclosure, an essential professional, legal and ethical obligation for pharmacists, enshrined in the Patient Safety (Notifiable Incidents and Open Disclosure) Act 2023 and the National Open Disclosure Framework (2023). This document aligns with the  requirements  of  these  documents  for  educational  providers  offering  clinical  education. Adherence to this open disclosure policy is important to maintain public trust, enhance patient care, and uphold the integrity of pharmacy education. For students, adherence ensures they are prepared to meet their professional responsibilities, including fostering trust, improving communication, and contributing to safer healthcare.

## 2. Purpose

Each qualified pharmacist must abide by the PSI Code of Conduct for Pharmacists, which details the professional principles, standards, ethical behaviours and principles for pharmacy practice. This is to ensure safe, legal, evidence based and ethical practice to the highest professional standards. The principles of open disclosure are deeply embedded in the PSI's  Core  Competency Framework for Pharmacists  and  the  Code  of  Conduct  for  Pharmacists.  These  guiding  documents  highlight  the requirement of open and honest communication, adherence to legal and ethical practice, and the importance of person-centered care. Together, they ensure that pharmacy professionals approach patient care with the highest standards of integrity, empathy, and accountability.

Students of the Master of Pharmacy (MPharm) must also abide by standards and a specific Code of Conduct. The SETU Master of Pharmacy Professional Code of Conduct for Student Pharmacists will be  formally  agreed  to  by  each  student  annually,  and  forms  part  of  evidence  of  the  student's commitment to fitness to practise. The code sets out standards of behaviour and professionalism, which students must adhere to across academic, clinical, and professional settings. Principle Seven of the code 'Be Open and Honest' addresses the student's role in Open Disclosure. It stipulates that students should 'Communicate in an open and honest manner when errors and near misses occur, acknowledging when things go wrong to academic and practice staff in all settings.' This principle emphasises transparency, accountability, and professionalism, aligning student responsibilities with the open disclosure framework. In accordance with this Policy, students are required to follow the relevant policy and procedure of the Experiential Learning Practice sites in relation to patient safety incidents.  For  the  avoidance  of  doubt,  students  must not  initiate  an  Open Disclosure but  should promptly inform their supervisor and participate as directed, under appropriate supervision.

The Department of Pharmacy at SETU is cognisant of the requirements and obligations outlined in the National Open Disclosure Framework (2023), particularly Section 5.4 (included in Appendix 1), and accepts responsibility for ensuring adherence to its requirements at both Departmental and student levels.

Requirements for Academic Institutes:

- Embed communication skills focused on open disclosure into all clinical/practical academic programmes.
- Ensure programmes adequately prepare graduates to understand and participate in open disclosure as part of professional practice.
- Require all graduates to undertake training on patient safety incidents and adverse event management as part of their education and training.
- Ensure graduates understand the legislative and policy frameworks, legal protections, and legal responsibilities related to open disclosure.
- Prepare  graduates  to  discuss  the  role  of  open  disclosure  in  the  provision  of  safe, patient/service user-centered, and effective health and social care services.
- Ensure all training sites delivering and supervising clinical/practice placements have an open disclosure policy aligned with the Framework principles.
- Require all academic and clinical staff who are responsible for clinical and practice education, both within the institution and at clinical sites, to have formal training in open disclosure.
- Ensure all students on placement have the opportunity to learn more about open disclosure in clinical practice.
- Provide  additional  support  for  any  students  or  trainees  involved  in  open  disclosure  and patient safety incidents or adverse events during placement or internship.

The department is committed to embedding open disclosure practices into the Master of Pharmacy programme, demonstrating its commitment to transparency, accountability, and fostering a culture of openness in pharmacy education and practice. These efforts will equip students and staff with the skills, knowledge, and values required to uphold the highest professional and ethical standards.

This policy ensures that open disclosure principles are embedded within the MPharm programme at SETU.

## 3. Scope

The policy applies to staff involved in designing and delivering clinical, patient care and pharmacy professional aspects of the Master of Pharmacy curriculum, management within the Department of Pharmacy,  and  Master  of  Pharmacy  students  through  their  clinical,  patient  care  and  pharmacy practice  educational  and  professional  activities.  It  ensures  alignment  with  the  National  Open Disclosure Framework (2023) and adherence to the highest standards of transparency, accountability, and professionalism in academic settings.

It  shall  be  made  generally  available  and  published  publicly via the  University  website.  It  will  be provided to students within their handbook and Virtual Learning Environment (VLE).

## 4. Principles

The guiding principles for the implementation of the SETU Master of Pharmacy Open Disclosure Policy are that they:

- a. Will be informed by national guidelines and align with best practice.
- b. Comply with national and EU legislation.
- c. Be developed in conjunction and align with existing SETU policies and regulations.
- d. Undergo regular monitoring and review to ensure that they remain fit-for purpose.
- e. Be communicated in a timely and effective manner.
- f. Be easily accessible and published on the relevant section of the SETU website.
- g. Be in keeping with the University's commitments to Equality, Diversity and Inclusion (EDI).

## 5. Definitions

For the purpose of the SETU Master of Pharmacy Open Disclosure Policy the following definitions apply:

Open Disclosure -The process of open, honest, and timely communication with patients, carers, and relevant persons following a notifiable incident or patient safety incident. This includes acknowledging when things go wrong, expressing regret, explaining what happened, and outlining actions taken to prevent recurrence. ( HSE Open Disclosure Policy, 2025) .

Patient  Safety  Incident -An  event  or  circumstance  that  resulted  in,  or  could  have  resulted  in, unintended or unanticipated harm to a patient during the provision of healthcare ( Patient Safety (Notifiable Incidents and Open Disclosure) Act 2023, as amended ).

Notifiable Incident -A specific, serious patient safety incident that must be mandatorily reported under  the Patient  Safety  (Notifiable  Incidents  and  Open  Disclosure)  Act  2023,  as  amended and managed through a formal open disclosure process.

National Open Disclosure Framework -The framework developed by the National Patient Safety Office to establish a structured, transparent approach to open disclosure across all health and social care settings in Ireland.

Preceptor/Tutor/Supervisor -A registered pharmacist in a placement site responsible for supervising, guiding, and supporting pharmacy students during their experiential learning placements.

The  Affiliation  for  Pharmacy  Practice  Experiential  Learning -  APPEL;  This  affiliation  manages  the experiential learning placements of the integrated pharmacy programmes of Schools of Pharmacy in Ireland.

## 6. Policy

## 6.1 Responsibilities of SETU, the Faculty of Science and Computing and the Department of Pharmacy

SETU  recognises  its  obligations  under  the  National  Open  Disclosure  Framework  (2023)  and  its important role as an academic institution in promoting transparency, professionalism, and patientcentered care in pharmacy education. These responsibilities for academic institutions are set out at Section 5.4 of the National Open Disclosure Framework (annexed hereto at Appendix 1) and reflected in the summation of responsibilities set out below. These responsibilities are central to preparing future pharmacists to uphold the highest standards of ethical and professional practice. To fulfil these obligations, the Faculty of Science and Computing and Department of Pharmacy are committed to ensuring  that  all  student  pharmacists  are  educated  in  a  supportive,  professional,  and  patientcentered  learning environment, and the MPharm will equip them with the knowledge, skills, and ethical values required to manage open disclosure effectively in their future roles as pharmacists.

The Department of Pharmacy will therefore:

1. Align Policies with the Framework :  Ensure all teaching Experiential Learning Practice sites maintain  open  disclosure  policies  aligned  with  the  National  Open  Disclosure  Framework (2023). promoting consistent and effective communication in patient safety incidents. For placements, assurance of this alignment will be obtained through the body responsible for the coordination of placements with placement providers.
2. Integrate Open Disclosure into the education curriculum : Embed open disclosure principles into  the  MPharm  curriculum,  with  a  focus  on  communication  skills,  legislative  and  policy frameworks, and ethical responsibility in accordance with the requirements of Section 5.4 of the National Open Disclosure Framework (2023).
3. Provide  Training :  Require  clinical  academic  staff,  students,  and  practice  educators  to complete  training  in  open  disclosure  practices,  including  managing  adverse  events  and patient safety incidents, and effective communication during an open disclosure process. The Department of Pharmacy will maintain a training record for staff and monitor ongoing training needs. For placements, assurance will be obtained through the coordinators of placements that  that  preceptors  have  undertaken  the  relevant  open  disclosure  training  to  effectively guide and support students in these practices.
4. Support  Students  during  Patient  Safety  Incidents  including  Notifiable  Incidents :  Offer structured  support,  including  debriefing  sessions,  counselling,  and  guidance,  for  students involved in patient safety incidents during placements.
5. Foster a Just Culture : Promote an environment where errors and near misses can be reported without fear of blame, encouraging transparency and continuous learning to improve patient care.
6. Monitor and Improve : Regularly evaluate the implementation of open disclosure policies to ensure alignment with national standards and address gaps in training, communication, and support mechanisms

7. Appoint an Open Disclosure Champion : Designate an Open Disclosure Champion within the Department to oversee the implementation of open disclosure practices, provide guidance to staff and students, and liaise with APPEL or equivalent/alternative, and placement sites to ensure consistent adherence to the Framework.
8. Establish Clear Responsibilities: Ensure that clear responsibilities between the Department of Pharmacy SETU and APPEL or equivalent/alternative, are established and maintained in relation  to  open  disclosure  practices.  This  includes  defining  roles  for  supporting  students during  placements,  managing  patient  safety  incidents,  and  ensuring  compliance  with  the Framework.  These  responsibilities  will  be  documented,  regularly  reviewed,  and  formally agreed upon to promote effective collaboration and support for all stakeholders.

## 6.2 Responsibilities of Master of Pharmacy Students

Master  of  Pharmacy  students  are  expected  to  maintain  the  principles  of  Open  Disclosure  in  all academic,  professional  and  clinical  settings.  These  obligations  align  with  the  National  Open Disclosure Framework (2023), the SETU Code of Conduct for Student Pharmacists, and the broader professional  standards  outlined  in  the  PSI  Core  Competency  Framework.  By  fulfilling  these obligations,  students  contribute  to  a  culture  of  transparency,  accountability,  and  continuous improvement in patient care. The students will therefore ensure they:

1. Adhere  to  the  SETU  Code  of  Conduct  for  Student  Pharmacists: Follow  the  professional standards  outlined  in  the  Code,  including  Principle  Seven:  Be  Open  and  Honest,  which requires students to: 'Communicate in an open and honest manner when errors and near misses  occur,  acknowledging  when  things  go  wrong  to  academic  and  practice  staff  in  all settings.'
2. Adhere to the SETU Charter and the SETU Student Expected Behaviour Policy: As students of SETU, MPharm students are required to abide by these University wide policies.
3. Engage in Open Disclosure Training: Actively participate in all training activities related to open disclosure, including communication skills, patient safety management, and ethical and legal practice.
4. Report Patient Safety Incidents: Promptly report any errors, near misses, or concerns to the appropriate academic or supervisor, in accordance with the policies and procedures of the Department of Pharmacy and placement sites. Students must not initiate Open Disclosure; instead,  they  must  promptly  inform  their  supervisor  and  participate  only  as  directed  and supervised in line with placement site's policy.
5. Follow Open Disclosure Policy: Comply with the open disclosure policies established by the Department of Pharmacy and clinical training sites to ensure clear and effective communication with patients, carers, and healthcare teams. Students must not initiate Open Disclosure;  instead,  they  must  promptly  inform  their  supervisor  and  participate  only  as directed and supervised in line with placement site's policy.

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  4  
 
1. 
Introduction/Context 
 
South East Technological University (SETU) is an equal opportunities employer committed to 
attracting, recruiting, developing and retaining the highest caliber of staff to build a 
diversified, supportive, innovative and inclusive workforce. This Policy sets out the framework for 
the application of a probationary period for staff members upon appointment to a new role 
within SETU. 
 
Probation is a structured official evaluation to confirm that a new staff member meets the 
required standards of performance in their role. The probation process is designed as a 
supportive mechanism to help a staff member assimilate into their role and be given every 
reasonable opportunity to achieve the required standards of performance through honest, 
fair, and constructive feedback.  
 
The probation period applicable to each role will be stated in each individual’s contract of 
employment and will reflect the duration of the contract. This period may be extended, where 
necessary and in accordance with employment legislation (see section 10 for further details) 
for a further defined period. Any such extension will be confirmed in writing.  
 
2. 
Purpose of Probation 
 
The purpose of probation is to provide a defined period during which a manager can assess a 
new employee’s suitability for continued employment, having regard to the required 
standards of performance for the role.  
 
Probation also provides the employee with an opportunity to demonstrate and maintain 
satisfactory standards of work, to gain a clear understanding of the expectations on the 
requirements of the role, to raise questions, and to receive constructive feedback and 
support.  
 
The policy provides managers with a structured framework to assess performance, assist in 
supporting the new staff member and identify and address any shortcomings in a fair and 
timely manner. 
 
3. 
Scope 
 
This policy applies to all new staff employed by the University. It provides a framework to 
ensure that expectations regarding the required standards of performance are clearly 
established and reviewed during the probation period. It shall be made generally available 
and published publicly via the University website. 
 
4. 
Aims of the Policy 
 
The aim of this Policy is to provide a formal review process for assessing a staff member’s 
suitability for a role during the probation period, having regard to the required standards of 
performance. Probation is intended to be a positive, collaborative and transparent process, 
which provides an opportunity to: 
 
• Allow the newly appointed staff member to assimilate into the role, Department and 
University. 
• Facilitate a clear understanding of their duties and roles in the University. 
• Support each manager in inducting new staff. 
• Provide appropriate information, guidance, training, and feedback to help staff meet 
required standards.  
• Identify and address any performance issues in a timely and constructive manner.

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  5  
 
• Enable a manager to make a recommendation in relation to a staff member’s 
suitability for continued employment in their role. 
 
5. 
Definitions 
 
• Probation Period - A defined timeframe during which a staff member’s performance 
and skills are reviewed to assess suitability for continued employment in the role. 
• Staff member - Newly appointed employee. 
• Manager - Person to whom the probationer reports. 
 
6. 
Policy 
 
The probation period recognises the initial period of employment as unique, requiring special 
attention from both the staff member and the University. Assessment of the individual's 
suitability for the post to which they have been appointed is an ongoing process during the 
probation period. This policy promotes the on-going and essential dialogue and assessment 
between manager and staff members and while formal probation meetings should take place 
at set times during this period this should not prevent or in any way discourage continuous 
and necessary day-to-day dialogue and engagement.  
 
7. 
Probation Process 
7.1 Probation Period Duration 
The duration of an individual’s probation period will be specified in their contract of 
employment. Any variation must be agreed in writing. The current probation timeframes are 
set out in Appendix 1 of this policy. Where a probation review meeting for an academic staff 
member fall during the academic holiday period, the probationary period will be paused 
during the academic leave period and will recommence at the start of the next academic term.  
 
7.2 Extension of Probation Period 
The University reserves the right to extend the probation period in limited circumstances in 
line with the University’s operational requirements and applicable employment legislation. 
Probation periods may be extended if the employee's performance, conduct, or overall 
suitability for the role is not deemed satisfactory during the initial probation period. In cases 
where a staff member is absent from work during their probation period due to Protective 
Leave, such as Maternity Leave, Paternity Leave, Adoptive Leave, Parent’s Leave, Carer’s Leave 
and statutory sick leave, the probationary period will be extended by a duration equivalent to 
the length of the absence. An extension may also be applied, on a proportionate basis, where 
the University has not had sufficient opportunity to fully assess performance or where there 
is a reasonable expectation that any identified performance issues can be overcome with 
further time and support. Any extension must be approved in consultation with HR and must 
comply with applicable employment legislation, including the European Union (Transparent 
and Predictable Working Conditions) Regulations 2022.  
7.3 Frequency of Probation Review Meetings 
The probationary process for a permanent post will normally comprise of three meetings 
conducted at regular intervals by the manager during the probation period. 
• Meeting one: ideally within the first 2 weeks of commencement in the post. 
• Meeting two: ideally by the midpoint of the probationary period. 
• Meeting three: ideally 6 weeks prior to the end of the probation period. 
• Please refer to Appendix 1 for the timelines for meetings. 
More frequent meetings may take place where specific issues arise. While the frequency may 
vary slightly, the principle of regular meetings should apply, which allows for an appropriate 
period of improvement or change.

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  6  
 
 
7.4 Probation Principles 
The following principles apply in relation to probation meetings: 
• The probation process is a mechanism for the manager to assess the suitability of the 
newly appointed staff member for the role having regard to required standards of 
performance, and to maintain regular communication with the staff member. 
• The process is also a mechanism for the staff members to gain feedback on their 
performance and talk to their manager about their experience of the role to date. This 
gives an opportunity for discussion on all aspects of the role, including whether 
additional support, training or adjustments are required. 
• The process is based on two-way dialogue between the manager and the staff member 
in relation to the staff member’s performance. 
• If aspects of a staff member’s performance are not satisfactory or could benefit from 
improvement, this needs to be clearly communicated to the staff member as early as 
possible in the process. An opportunity for improvement and reasonable support 
should be provided. 
• Staff members are expected to meet the required standards of performance and, 
where concerns are raised, take responsibility for an improvement in their 
performance. Staff members are also encouraged to bring to the attention of the 
manager any issues or additional support/training they consider relevant to their 
performance or assimilation into the role. 
• While the staff member has a right of reply in relation to each of the performance 
reviews, the manager will make the final recommendation on the outcome, in 
consultation with HR, based on a fair and objective assessment in accordance with this 
policy and applicable employment legislation. 
. 
The probation process will ordinarily be overseen by the relevant manager. In exceptional 
circumstances, the review may be conducted by another appropriate manager 
7.5 Outcomes 
Recommendations regarding the outcome of the probation process will be made by the 
relevant manager. 
• Successful Completion - Where the recommendation is that the staff member is 
deemed to have passed their probation, they will receive written confirmation from 
their manager. 
• Extension - Where the recommendation is that the staff member’s probation period 
should be extended, the staff member will be advised in writing as to the reasons, 
necessary improvements required (where applicable) and duration of the extension 
by their manager. Extensions will only be applied in limited circumstances where 
reasonable and proportionate and where the University requires additional time to 
assess performance or where assessment has been paused (for e.g. due to protected 
leave or significant absence). Any extension must be approved in consultation with HR 
and must comply with applicable employment legislation including the European 
Union (Transparent and Predictable Working Conditions) Regulations 2022.  
 
• Failure of Probation – Where, following review meetings, significant concerns remain 
regarding performance or overall suitability for the role, these should be discussed 
with the staff member at the earliest opportunity.  Employment may be terminated at 
any point during, or at the conclusion of, the probationary period where the required 
standards are not met. The staff member may be accompanied at any formal 
probation review meeting by a work or a trade union representative. Any decision to 
terminate an employee’s contract of employment is a serious matter and will only be 
taken after the staff member has been given clear feedback during the probation 
period, an opportunity to improve, and the right to respond at each stage.  In such 
cases, the manager should contact HR without delay. HR, in conjunction with the 
manager, will notify the staff member of the outcome of the probation process and,

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  7  
 
where applicable, provide formal notice of termination of contract or pay in lieu of 
notice in accordance with the contract of employment and applicable legislation. The 
employee will be provided with the necessary support and information regarding the 
outcome. 
 
8. 
Responsibility and Authority 
 
The effectiveness of the probation process can be significantly improved by regular, honest 
and constructive dialogue between the manager and the staff member. Therefore, both the 
staff member and the manager should prepare for the probation process by familiarising 
themselves with this Policy and associated procedures. 
 
Staff Member 
The roles and responsibilities of the staff member within the probationary process are 
detailed below. 
 
• Understand and take responsibility for meeting required performance standards and 
for future development. 
• Prepare adequately in advance of probation meetings. 
• Fully engage with process and respond with feedback. 
• Ensure that the expectations and duties of the role are understood and seek 
clarification when necessary. 
• Understand the overall work objectives / duties. 
• Deliver on objectives / milestones for the full probationary period. 
• Be self-motivating and identify your development needs within the context of the role 
and how they may be addressed as early as possible. 
• Attend any training courses that have been identified as a requirement or support. 
• Respond constructively to feedback and apply any suggested improvements. 
• Raise any concerns or supports required to carry out the role effectively.  
 
Manager 
The roles and responsibilities of the manager within the probation process are outlined below. 
• Set clear objectives and milestones for staff members at the start of their probation 
period. 
• Provide training and ongoing support to the staff member during the probation 
period. 
• Conduct Probation Meetings for their own team members and complete the required 
Probation Meeting Forms. 
• Communicate with staff where difficulties arise with their performance and agree with 
the performance improvement plan.  
• Document all feedback and actions agreed.  
• Notify HR of extended absences which may require an extension of the probation 
period. 
• Notify staff members of any extension(s) to the probationary period with reasons and 
duration. HR should be notified prior to any extensions. 
• Liaise with HR where significant concerns arise or where failure of probation is being 
considered. 
 
Human Resources 
• Provide training to the manager to complete the probation process. 
• Support managers and staff members as required in the process. 
• HR Business Partners will provide guidance where recommendations for failure / 
extension / pausing of probation are required, ensuring compliance with applicable 
employment legislation and the University policy.

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  8  
 
• Ensure the outcome of the process is communicated to the staff member in writing. 
 
9. 
Compliance 
 
All staff of the University are subject to compliance with this policy. 
 
10. 
Related Documents 
 
• 
European Union (Transparent and Predictable Working Conditions) Regulations 2022 
• 
Unfair Dismissals Acts 1977–2015. 
• 
Minimum Notice and Terms of Employment Acts 1973–2005 
• 
Employment Equality Acts 1998–2021 
  
 
11. 
Useful Links 
 
SETU HR Policies  
Probation Cognito Meeting Form  
Probation Policy Guidance Document 
 
 
12. 
Policy Review 
This Policy will be reviewed every 3 years and in line with appropriate legislation and/or 
circulars or updates as issued by Department of Public Expenditure, NDP Delivery & Reform, 
Higher Education Authority or Department of Further and Higher Education, Research, 
Innovation and Science, from time to time. 
 
13. 
Appendices

Probation Policy 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  9

Where a probation review meeting for an academic staff member falls during the academic

<a name="procedures-for-managing-allegations-of-misconduct-in-research"></a>
## Procedures for Managing Allegations of Misconduct in Research

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  3  
 
1. 
Introduction/Context 
 
South East Technological University (SETU) is committed to ensuring the highest levels of 
research integrity in its community. Research integrity is crucial to preserving the 
trustworthiness of the research system and its results. Misconduct in research can have 
serious consequences for individual researchers, employers, funders, research participants, 
and the wider public, and can damage public trust in research and public research institutions 
and it can result in significant financial costs for funding institutions. Despite this, it is possible 
that some researchers are unfamiliar with what it is and how it is managed. SETU has clear 
standards on what constitutes research misconduct outlined in a Code of Conduct for 
Responsible Research Practice and the different forms of research misconduct that can occur. 
The Code of Conduct also guides all SETU researchers on research integrity, research ethics 
and relevant legislation and regulations that affect research integrity. These Procedures for 
Managing Allegations of Misconduct in Research should be used in conjunction with the Code 
of Conduct for Responsible Research Practice and the National Policy Statement on Ensuring 
Research Integrity in Ireland1, both of which list the different forms of research misconduct 
that can occur. 
 
2. 
Purpose 
 
This document sets out in detail the procedures to be followed when responding to an 
allegation of research misconduct. It also outlines how a concern or complaint should be 
raised and the different stages involved in assessing, initiating and investigating the 
complaint. This is a requirement of the European Code of Conduct for Research integrity2 and 
is also laid out in the National Policy Statement on Ensuring Research Integrity in Ireland. 
These procedures also take account of the National Forum on Research Integrity Guidelines 
for the Investigation of Misconduct in Research3 and the UK Research Integrity Office’s 
(UKRIO) Procedures for the Investigations of Misconduct in Research4. It ensures that the 
integrity of our research is protected by dealing properly with cases of misconduct where they 
arise. These procedures ensure that all allegations of research misconduct are handled in a 
 
1 Irish Universities Association, ‘Policy Statement on Ensuring Research Integrity in Ireland’ (Revised Edition 
2024) 
https://www.iua.ie/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/National-Policy-Statement-on-Ensuring-Research-
Integrity-in-Ireland-Dec-2024.pdf. 
2 All European Academies, ‘European Code of Conduct for Research Integrity’ (Revised Edition 2023) 
https://allea.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/European-Code-of-Conduct-Revised-Edition-2023.pdf. 
3 Irish Universities Association, ‘National Forum on Research Integrity Guidelines for the Investigation of 
Misconduct 
in 
Research’ 
(2016) 
https://www.iua.ie/wp-content/uploads/2019/08/Guidelines-for-RI-
investigations-FINAL-17-08-16-1.pdf.  
4 UK Research Integrity Office, Procedures for the Investigation of Misconduct in Research (2023) 
https://ukrio.org/wp-content/uploads/UKRIO-Procedure-for-the-Investigation-of-Misconduct-in-Research-
V2.pdf.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  4  
 
transparent, uniform, comprehensive, objective and expedient manner, and in a manner that 
ensures natural justice for all parties.  
3. 
Scope 
 
This document is applicable to all individuals and groups who carry out or support research 
activities under the auspices of SETU. The procedure applies to all permanent, part-time or 
contract staff employed by SETU engaged in research, or in the setting of research priorities, 
strategies, policies and the assessment of research on any SETU campuses or on behalf of 
SETU at any location (including secondments to other higher-level institutions). For the 
purposes of this document, the term SETU Researcher refers to: 
• 
All research active staff at the University, 
• 
including postdoctoral researchers and research fellows, 
• 
Postgraduate research students, 
• 
All visiting and seconded research staff and visiting postgraduate research 
students,  
• 
Holders of associated, adjunct and emeritus positions in SETU, 
• 
Internal 
supervisors 
of 
postgraduate 
research  
students, 
• 
Technical, professional, managerial, administrative and all support staff 
involved in research-related activities, 
• 
All individuals engaged in the setting of research priorities, strategies, 
policies and the assessment of research. 
Undergraduate/postgraduate taught students: Allegations of research misconduct 
involving undergraduate students and postgraduate taught students are addressed solely 
under the SETU Student Academic Misconduct Policy and Disciplinary Procedure. 
Postgraduate research students: These procedures apply to postgraduate research students 
if the allegation relates to research integrity and misconduct (e.g. poor data management, 
misuse of research project funds, authorship conflicts, failure to get ethical approval etc.). 
Allegations of academic misconduct, that is misconduct related to the assessed elements of a 
programme (e.g. exams, assignments, thesis) come under the SETU Student Academic 
Misconduct Policy and Disciplinary Procedure.  
As stated in the SETU Academic Misconduct Policy and Disciplinary Procedure (S.1.4)5, the 
Research Integrity Officer (RIO) and the Complaints Resolution Officer (CRO) will determine 
 
5 As stated in the SETU Academic Misconduct Policy and Disciplinary Procedure (S.1.4): “Where a matter relating 
to research integrity involving a Student or a matter relating to a potential breach of the Academic Misconduct

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  5  
 
on a case-by-case basis whether an allegation should be handled under these procedures or 
the SETU Student Academic Misconduct procedures, or both procedures. This will depend on 
the status of the researcher (e.g. student, staff member or both student and employee) and 
the alleged nature of the allegation of research misconduct.  
If it is handled under both procedures, only one disciplinary procedure can apply at the end. 
In the case of postgraduate research students, the SETU Academic Misconduct Policy and 
Disciplinary Procedure will apply. In the case of researchers who are members of staff, the 
Institutes of Technology Disciplinary Procedure will apply. 
 
4. 
Principles  
 
Based on the principles outlined in the National Policy Statement on Ensuring Research 
Integrity in Ireland (p.13) and the European Code of Conduct on Research Integrity (p.11-12), 
the following principles will be incorporated into any investigation process: 
4.1 Those parties involved in the procedure must ensure that any interests they have which 
might constitute a conflict of interest are disclosed and managed in line with SETU’s Conflict 
of Interest Policy;  
4.2 Investigations are required to be fair, comprehensive and conducted expediently, 
ensuring accuracy, objectivity and thoroughness; 
4.3 Investigation of research misconduct allegations should be conducted in a manner that is 
fair to all parties and in accordance with relevant laws; 
4.4 Procedures will be conducted confidentially to protect all those involved in the 
investigation; 
4.5 Detailed and confidential records will be maintained on all aspects of the procedure in 
line with General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) and any amendments thereto; 
4.6 Where possible, any disclosure to third parties (e.g. HR) should be made on a confidential 
basis; 
4.7 If SETU and/or its staff have legal obligations to inform third parties (e.g. external funding 
agencies, industry partners) of research misconduct allegations, those obligations must be 
fulfilled at the appropriate time through the correct mechanisms; 
 
Policy by a postgraduate research Student arises, which gives rise to an allegation of Academic Misconduct or 
Serious Academic Misconduct under this Academic Misconduct Policy, the CRO will consult with the Research 
Integrity Officer on the matter” (page 10).

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  6  
 
4.8 Anyone accused of research misconduct is presumed innocent until proven otherwise. No 
person should suffer any unnecessary penalty when accused of Research Misconduct before 
the allegation is proven; 
4.9 Persons accused of research misconduct will be given full details of the allegation(s) in 
writing and allowed a fair process for responding and to have a representative or work 
colleague present for any meeting or interview associated with the investigation; 
4.10 No person should suffer any penalty to their career before, during or after an 
investigation for making a bona fide allegation of research misconduct in good faith, but 
disciplinary action should be taken against persons found to have made allegations in bad 
faith (i.e. if it is made with reckless disregard for or wilful ignorance of facts that would 
disprove the allegation); 
4.11 Proportionate action should be taken against persons found to have committed research 
misconduct; 
4.12 Any action(s) taken should be subject to right of appeal; 
4.13 All parties involved should be provided with information on available supports during 
the complaint’s procedure.  
 
5. 
Definitions 
 
Allegation An allegation means a written statement of possible research misconduct made to 
the Research Integrity Officer (RIO). An allegation is not in good faith if it is made with reckless 
disregard for or wilful ignorance of facts that would disprove the allegation. If someone else 
in the organisation receives an allegation of possible research misconduct involving a SETU 
researcher, they shall inform the RIO in writing. If any individual in the organisation receives 
an allegation of possible research misconduct involving an SETU researcher through a third-
party source such as a peer reviewer, a publisher, a funding agency, industry partner, another 
university etc., they shall also inform the RIO in writing.  
Complainant means a person who makes an allegation of research misconduct. The 
Complainant may be a person who has been impacted by the reported behaviour, witnessed 
it, or become aware of the alleged behaviour through other means. Any person or persons, 
whether employed by SETU or not, may make an allegation of research misconduct under 
these procedures. Complainants will normally put their name to any allegations they make. 
However, it is recognised that complainants can be concerned about revealing their identity. 
Allegations raised which are anonymous, or matters identified where there is no specific 
complainant, will be considered at the discretion of the RIO, taking into account the 
seriousness of the concerns raised, and the extent of supporting evidence provided. Where 
appropriate, advice will be sought, and consideration given to whether the respondent will

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  7  
 
be able to defend themselves6. Depending on the nature of the allegation (e.g., if the 
allegation of research misconduct relates to an alleged offence), then complainants may 
instead consider submitting their complaint under SETU’s Protected Disclosures Policy7. 
Conflict of Interest means an actual, perceived or potential conflict where the personal 
interests of a person may be in conflict (or perceived to be in conflict) with the interests of 
SETU. See SETU’s Conflict of Interest Policy for a more extensive definition. 
Inquiry An inquiry (stage 1) means gathering information and initial fact-finding to determine 
whether an allegation or apparent instance of research misconduct warrants an investigation.  
Investigation An investigation (stage 2) means the formal examination and evaluation of all 
relevant facts to determine if misconduct has occurred and, if so, to determine the 
responsible person and the seriousness of the misconduct. 
Research Integrity Officer: The RIO is the individual employed by the University to receive 
allegations of research misconduct. They determine whether allegations received would, if 
upheld, fit within the definition of Research Misconduct as per the SETU Code of Conduct for 
Responsible Research Practice. The RIO initiates and oversees the procedures for 
investigating allegations of research misconduct and ensures confidentiality of the process. 
They maintain records of the process and outcomes of investigations of allegations of 
research misconduct, and they keep the Complainant and Respondent up to date on 
investigation proceedings and communicating findings of the Inquiry Panel and Investigation 
Panel, as relevant, and issuing correspondence to the Complainant and the Respondent. They 
report on the investigation to relevant internal contacts and, where appropriate, in 
coordination with the Vice-President for Research, Innovation and Impact (VP RII), to external 
organisations (e.g., funding bodies, publishers, industry partners). 
Research integrity refers to all the factors that underpin good research practice and promote 
trust and confidence in the research process. Research integrity covers all disciplines of 
research and all sectors where research is carried out. Research integrity encompasses all 
research and the whole lifecycle of research, from the initial idea and design of the 
project/study, the writing of the proposal and application for funding, through to the conduct 
of the research and its dissemination. It also covers making sure that environments and 
systems for research safeguard and enhance good research practice, rather than hinder it – 
often described as ‘research culture‘. 
Research misconduct can be defined as behaviours that deliberately or recklessly fall short of 
standards expected in the conduct of research. An offence constitutes research misconduct 
if it affects the integrity of the research record. The most serious misconduct behaviours 
include fabrication, falsification, or plagiarism in proposing, performing, or reviewing 
research, or in reporting research results. However, there are other unacceptable research 
practices that seriously deviate from those that are commonly accepted within the research 
 
6 The definition of complainant and the wording of anonymous complaints is taken from the UK Research 
Integrity Office’s Procedures for the Investigation of Misconduct in Research (p.16, 2023). 
7 SETU Researchers should refer to Section 5.1 of SETU’s Protected Disclosures Policy for the full list of offences 
that could be considered under this policy.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  8  
 
community for proposing, conducting, or reporting research, and serious or repeated 
examples of such practices can constitute research misconduct. Research misconduct does 
not include honest error or honest differences in the design, execution, interpretation or 
judgment in evaluating research methods or results, or misconduct unrelated to the research 
process. Similarly, it does not include poor research per se unless there is an intention to 
deceive. Please refer to SETU’s Code of Conduct for Responsible Research Practice and the 
National Policy Statement on Ensuring Research Integrity in Ireland, which outline an 
extensive list of behaviours that could be considered research misconduct.  
Respondent means the person or persons against whom an allegation of research misconduct 
is directed or the person whose actions are the subject of the inquiry or investigation. There 
can be more than one Respondent in any inquiry or investigation. Where there is more than 
one Respondent, each may be separately or jointly considered in the inquiry stage or the 
investigation stage and may be considered jointly or individually in any resulting report. The 
Inquiry Panel will determine whether multiple respondents should be addressed jointly or 
individually at either stage or in the resulting reports. 
 
6. 
Handling suspected research misconduct  
 
 
6.1 Receipt of complaint, initial assessment & initiation of inquiry process 
 
6.1.1 The purpose of the Receipt of Allegations Stage is to assess an allegation of research 
misconduct that has been received and to determine whether the matter falls under the 
institutional procedure for investigating misconduct in research (in terms of both the matter 
raised and the individuals identified) and the most appropriate course of action for dealing 
with the allegation. Its aim is NOT to investigate the substance of the matter raised.  
 
6.1.2 Any postgraduate research student, researcher, individual or external body (e.g., a 
funding agency or a publisher) who believes that an act of research misconduct has occurred 
should make a complaint in writing through the completion and submission of a Complaint 
Form to the RIO at research.integrity@setu.ie. If supporting evidence is available, the 
complainant should also submit this along with the complaint form. The complaint form can 
be downloaded here and a copy is provided in Appendix 1 of these procedures. 
 
6.1.3 Any postgraduate research student, researcher, individual (e.g., head of faculty) or 
external body receiving an allegation about research misconduct should refer the 
complainant to the Research Integrity Officer (RIO).

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  9  
 
6.1.4 Following receipt of a written complaint, the RIO will acknowledge receipt at an early 
stage of an allegation by the Complainant in writing, informing them that the allegation will 
be considered initially under the 'Receipt of Allegations' stage of the Procedure. A copy of the 
Procedure will be provided to the Complainant. 
 
6.1.5 Allegations which are anonymous or where there is no specific complainant will only be 
considered at the discretion of the Research Integrity Officer (RIO), taking into account: the 
seriousness of the concerns raised, the extent of supporting evidence provided and the 
likelihood of confirming the complaint from alternative credible sources/evidence.  Where 
appropriate, advice will be sought (e.g. SETU’s Legal Counsel), and consideration given to 
whether the respondent will be able to defend themselves. 
 
6.1.6 Complainants should be aware that making an unsubstantiated or malicious misconduct 
allegation is considered a form of research misconduct as outlined in SETU’s Code of Conduct 
for Responsible Research Practice and could be subject to disciplinary procedure. 
 
6.1.7 Whilst there is no deadline for the submission of a complaint, a delay in making a 
complaint may limit the ability of SETU to address matters which form the basis of a 
complaint. However, complaints related to alleged research misconduct that occurred many 
years ago shall be judged by the standards and policies for investigating allegations of 
research misconduct prevailing at the date that the alleged behaviour took place. 
 
6.1.8 SETU will investigate and consider complaints in a timely manner (see Appendix 2 for an 
overview of key timelines), providing reasonable deadlines at each stage of the process. 
However, delays may occur where the case is complex or individuals relevant to the 
investigation are not available to attend meetings. All relevant parties will be provided with 
written updates and reasons where there is a delay. 
 
6.1.9 The RIO shall be free to seek confidential advice from persons with relevant expertise, 
both within the organisation and outside it to assess an allegation of research misconduct 
that has been received and to determine whether the matter falls under the institutional 
procedure for investigating misconduct in research, or another policy or procedure. 
 
6.1.10 This procedure acknowledges that cases of research misconduct may need to be 
handled in conjunction with other university policies and departments/offices. Nothing in this 
procedure shall prohibit allegations of misconduct in research being investigated jointly or 
separately under this procedure and under an alternative policy or procedure. However, only 
one disciplinary procedure may apply if an allegation of research misconduct is upheld.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 0  
 
 
6.1.11 In cases where the RIO considers it appropriate to do so (e.g., misunderstandings, poor 
research or poor supervisory practice), they may recommend a process of informal resolution 
or other non-disciplinary measures (e.g., education and training) having regard to the matters 
at issue. 
 
6.1.12 Every effort will be made to maintain strict confidentiality and discretion in the 
processing and investigation of a complaint. Where it is necessary to meet with persons, the 
importance of confidentiality will be emphasised to the relevant parties. In this regard, all 
parties will be required to respect the privacy of everyone involved and confidentiality of the 
process by refraining from discussing the matter with others. 
 
6.1.13 When an allegation is first received, the RIO will first check that the allegation falls 
within the definition of research misconduct as outlined in the SETU’s Code of Conduct for 
Responsible Research Practice, and that there is sufficient information to initiate a Stage 1 
Initial Inquiry: fact gathering exercise. If it does, then the matter will be addressed via the 
inquiry process below. If it does not, the process will conclude at this point and the 
Complainant, where they are identifiable, should normally be informed within 10 working 
days8 of any alternative SETU policy or procedure through which their complaint could be 
pursued. It should be clear that the RIO’s responsibilities with respect to processing the 
allegations have concluded at this point. 
 
6.1.14 The RIO may decide that it is necessary to contact the Complainant and/or the 
Respondent to seek information or ask questions to carry out the initial assessment. Such 
contact should be in writing; the Complainant and Respondent would not normally be 
interviewed at this stage. If it is necessary to contact the Respondent, they should first be 
informed that allegation(s) of research misconduct have been made concerning them and 
that the allegation(s) is being assessed to determine what if any action should be taken. 
 
6.1.15 Following this initial assessment of the complaint and if the RIO determines that there 
is sufficient information to initiate an inquiry and that the complaint falls under this 
procedure, the person accused of research misconduct will be given full details of the 
allegation(s) in writing and that an inquiry process and inquiry panel is being initiated. They 
will also be provided with information on the procedures for the inquiry into the allegation 
 
8 For the purposes of this Procedure, “working days” means Monday to Friday, excluding weekends, public 
holidays, and university closure days.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 1  
 
and they will be invited to provide a written response to the allegations as soon as possible 
but no later than 10 working days.   
6.1.16 When allegations have been made against more than one Respondent, the RIO should 
inform each individual separately and not divulge the identity of any other Respondent. 
 
6.1.17 The RIO will then inform the Complainant, formally and in writing, of the conclusions 
of the review of the allegation(s) and an outline of the next steps. 
 
6.1.18 Following the initial assessment of the complaint and the decision to initiate an inquiry 
process, the RIO shall notify the Vice President for Research Innovation and Impact (VP RII) 
that an allegation has been made, the basis for the allegation and the name of the person 
against whom the allegation has been made. These persons must maintain the confidentiality 
of the inquiry process.  
 
6.1.19 It is expected that all relevant parties involved in the Complaint will appropriately and 
respectfully engage with the RIO throughout the process and follow the procedures properly. 
 
6.1.20 SETU is cognisant that the Procedure itself can be very stressful for all those involved. 
Respondents and Complainants and, where appropriate, witnesses will receive information 
at every step on how to access available supports during the process.  
 
6.1.21 The RIO shall consult with HR to determine the contractual status of the Respondent if 
relevant. Equally the RIO shall consult with student offices to determine the status of a 
postgraduate research student if relevant.  
 
6.1.22 The above steps should be addressed within a reasonable timeframe after receipt of 
the allegation - within 20 working days. 
 
6.1.23 Any parties involved in these procedures must declare any conflict of interest that may 
arise during an inquiry or an investigation in line with SETU’s Conflict of Interest Policy.  
 
6.1.24 In the case where the RIO is the subject of the allegation or has a conflict of interest, 
or is absent, in relation to a complaint, the case should be referred to the Vice President for 
Research Innovation and Impact and they will nominate a delegated officer to fulfil the role 
of the RIO in this inquiry and the setting up of an inquiry panel. This will be decided on a case-
by-case basis.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 2  
 
 
6.2 Stage 1 Initial Inquiry: fact gathering exercise 
 
6.2.1 The purpose of stage 1 initial inquiry (a fact gathering exercise) is to make a preliminary 
evaluation of the available evidence and to hear the testimony of the Respondent, 
Complainant, and any key witnesses. The purpose of the inquiry is not to reach a final 
conclusion about whether misconduct definitely occurred or who was responsible. Instead, it 
seeks to determine whether there is sufficient evidence of possible research misconduct or 
sufficiently serious research misconduct to warrant an investigation, which would mean 
proceeding to stage 2.  
 
6.2.2 Once a stage 1 initial inquiry process is initiated the RIO may sequester any research 
records that are necessary for the inquiry. The RIO should ensure insofar as is possible that 
all relevant information and evidence are secured so that the Stage 1 Initial Inquiry Panel and 
any subsequent Stage 2 Investigation Panel can have access to them. Copies should also be 
provided to the Respondent. 
 
6.2.3 The RIO will appoint a panel. The panel should consist of at least three members, and 
they should be peer researchers, administrators or other qualified persons and the members 
should be a mix of internal and external persons (at least one external person). The RIO will 
select one of the members of the Inquiry Panel to act as its Chair. Signed declarations of 
confidentiality and conflicts of interest will be obtained from all those involved in the inquiry. 
 
6.2.4 The Inquiry Panel should continue with the inquiry irrespective of the Complainant 
withdrawing the allegations, the Respondent admitting to the allegations, the Complainant 
or Respondent resigning or where the respondent refuses to engage with the process. 
 
6.2.5 The RIO shall set out terms of reference for the Inquiry Panel. 
 
6.2.6 The respondent and complainant shall be informed of the composition of the panel. The 
Respondent and Complainant may raise with the RIO concerns that they may have about the 
persons chosen to carry out the Stage 1 Initial Inquiry but neither has a right of veto over 
those nominated. The RIO will consider any concerns raised and whether new persons should 
be selected to carry out the Stage 1 Inquiry. 
 
6.2.7 The Inquiry Panel will normally interview the Complainant, Respondent and any key 
witnesses, as well as examining relevant research records and materials.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 3  
 
 
6.2.8 Complainants and Respondents can be accompanied to interviews by a representative 
or work colleague. 
 
6.2.9 It is expected that all relevant parties involved in the Complaint will appropriately and 
respectfully engage with the Inquiry Panel and the RIO throughout the Stage 1 Inquiry and 
follow the procedures properly. 
 
6.2.10 Within 30 working days of its first meeting, the Inquiry Panel must produce a draft 
preliminary inquiry report (a stage 1 report) that states the names and titles of the panel 
members and external experts, if any; the allegations; a summary of the inquiry process used; 
a list of the research records reviewed; summaries of any interviews; a description of the 
evidence in sufficient detail to demonstrate whether an investigation is warranted or not; and 
the panel’s determination as to whether:  
i) 
stage 2 (investigation) should be initiated or not;  
ii) 
and whether any other actions should be taken if stage 2 is not to be initiated. For 
example, the stage 1 report may determine that an allegation has substance, but 
that, prima facie, the misconduct is insufficiently grave to warrant a full 
investigation. It may be decided to address the issue through education and 
training, in consultation with the Respondent's line manager.  
6.2.11 In the event of unavoidable delays in gathering testimony and/or other evidence the 
duration of the panel’s work can be extended on request to the RIO. Any delays to this 
timescale will be explained to the Complainant, the Respondent and the RIO in writing, 
presenting an estimated revised date of completion. 
6.2.12 The RIO will provide the Respondent with a copy of the draft stage 1 report for 
comment and rebuttal and will provide the Complainant with portions of the draft stage 1 
report that address the Complainant's role and opinions in the investigation. 
6.2.13 The Complainant and Respondent will provide their comments, if any, to the Inquiry 
Panel within 15 working days of their receipt of the draft stage 1 report. In response to 
exceptional circumstances, the RIO may extend this time limit. Any comments that the 
Complainant or Respondent submits on the draft report will become part of the final stage 1 
report and record. Based on the comments, the Inquiry Panel may revise the draft stage 1 
report as appropriate. 
6.2.14 The RIO may also ask the Inquiry Panel to clarify any of its findings in the draft stage 1 
report. 
6.2.15 Once the panel has received any comments submitted by the Complainant and 
Respondent, and has made any revisions to the draft report, it will finalise the stage 1 report 
accordingly.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 4  
 
6.2.16 Stage 1 is complete when the panel submits its final stage 1 report to the RIO. The RIO 
will review the stage 1 report. Where the Inquiry Panel has concluded that stage 2 (an 
investigation) should be initiated, this decision is subject to the RIO being satisfied that 
procedures have been adequately followed and that measures are in place to ensure natural 
justice and fair procedures are observed. 
6.2.17 The RIO will provide a copy of the final report to the Respondent and Complainant, 
advise both parties whether or not the process will proceed to stage 2 (an investigation), and 
will notify appropriate institutional officials (e.g., VP RII) of the preliminary inquiry report's 
conclusion. 
6.2.18 If the Inquiry Panel determines that the allegations are sufficiently serious and have 
sufficient substance to justify a formal investigation, the RIO will convene a Stage 2 
Investigation Panel and the case will progress to Phase 2 of this procedure. 
6.2.19 If the Inquiry Panel determines that the allegations are not sufficiently serious or not 
well-founded, or that for any other reason they should not progress any further, the case will 
not progress to Stage 2 Full Investigation Stage of this procedure. The RIO will take such 
steps/actions as they consider as appropriate to the seriousness of the allegations, to support 
the reputation of the Respondent, the research project and the research environment. 
 
6.3 Stage 2: Full Investigation Stage 
 
6.3.1 The purpose of Stage 2 Full Investigation Stage is to review all the relevant evidence 
and:  
a. conclude whether an allegation of misconduct in research is upheld in full, upheld in 
part or not upheld;  
b. and make recommendations as appropriate, for consideration by the appropriate 
Organisational authorities, regarding any further action that the Full Investigation 
Panel ("the Investigation Panel") deems necessary to address any misconduct it may 
have found (e.g., this could include recommendations to correct the record of 
research/withdraw a publication, address other matters uncovered during its work, 
referral to a disciplinary procedure, referral to another policy, notify particular 
authorities and sponsors etc.). 
6.3.2 The RIO shall establish an Investigation Panel with formal terms of reference within 20 
working days following receipt of the final report of the Inquiry Panel in Stage 1.  
6.3.3 The Investigation Panel should consist of at least three members, and the RIO will select 
one member to act as Chair. Additionally, the panel members should: 
i) have relevant skills and knowledge in the area of research in which the misconduct has 
alleged to have taken place.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 5  
 
ii) not be from the same faculty or research centre where the research misconduct is alleged 
to have taken place.  
iii) Be comprised of at least one member that is external to the university. 
iv) Not have been members of the stage 1 Inquiry Panel.  
v) Declare and manage any conflicts of interest. 
(vi) Maintain confidentiality unless otherwise required by law or by SETU. 
 
6.3.4 The respondent and complainant shall be informed of the composition of the 
Investigation Panel. The Respondent and Complainant may raise with the RIO concerns that 
they may have about the persons chosen to carry out the Full Investigation but neither has a 
right of veto over those nominated. The RIO will consider any concerns raised and whether 
new persons should be selected to carry out the Stage 2 Full Investigation. 
 
6.3.5 Once initiated, the Investigation Panel should follow its course, even in the situation of 
the Complainant withdrawing the allegations, the Respondent admitting to the allegations or 
the Complainant or Respondent resigning. 
6.3.6 If required to facilitate a full and fair investigation and/or the operation of any aspect of 
this Procedure, the RIO, and the Investigation Panel conducting the Full Investigation shall be 
free to seek confidential advice from persons with relevant expertise, both within the 
Organisation and outside it. Those seeking advice will, so far as is possible, anonymise the 
information provided to make no information available which could lead to the identification 
of the Complainant, Respondent or other individuals involved in the case. Persons consulted 
will be subject to the same requirements on confidentiality as others involved in the process. 
6.3.7 Persons who might be consulted include but are not limited to:  
a. experts in particular disciplines of research; or  
b. experts in particular aspects of the conduct of research, such as members of research ethics 
committees, statisticians, editors of academic journals or equivalent persons from relevant 
areas of dissemination in research; and/or experts in addressing misconduct in research and 
poor practice; or  
c. representatives of Organisational departments such as: Legal Services, Human Resources, 
Student Services, Finance; Governance/Registry, Research Office, Health and Safety Office, 
Library Services, Information and Technology Services or the equivalents; or  
d. legal advisers. 
6.3.8 It may also be determined during Stage 2 Full Investigation whether there are additional 
instances of possible misconduct that would justify broadening the scope beyond the initial 
allegations.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 6  
 
6.3.9 Should any evidence or allegation arise during the formal investigation that suggests 
other instances of potential misconduct by the Respondent or potential misconduct in 
research by another person, then the Investigation Panel should submit these new allegations 
to the RIO in writing. This submission should be accompanied by supporting evidence and/or 
details of the alleged misconduct. The RIO shall decide what action, if any, is necessary. The 
terms of reference for the Investigation Panel would be amended accordingly. 
6.3.10 Concurrently, the RIO should notify the Respondent of any such developments. Such 
actions may include, but are not limited to, expansion of the scope of the existing Research 
Investigation Panel; the initiation of a new investigation under this procedure; and/or referral 
to another SETU policy (e.g. SETU Protected Disclosures, SETU Dignity & Respect Policy).  
6.3.11 The findings of the Investigation Panel will be set forth in a written investigation report 
(a stage 2 report). 
6.3.12 The investigation will normally involve examination of all documentation including, but 
not necessarily limited to, relevant research records, computer files, financial records, 
proposals, manuscripts, publications, correspondence, memoranda, and notes of telephone 
calls. Additional research records not previously considered may be sequestered by the panel. 
This Investigation Panel shall ensure that this is GDPR compliant. 
6.3.13 The Investigation Panel shall consult with the RIO on the specific question of how to 
ensure natural justice and fair procedures are observed as part of the process. Whenever 
possible, the panel will interview the Complainant, the Respondent, and other individuals 
who might have information regarding aspects of the allegations. Interviews may be taped or 
transcribed. Any such recordings must be GDPR compliant. 
6.3.14 The Respondent may take appropriate advice and have a representative or a work 
colleague present during investigation interviews. 
6.3.15 The Investigation Panel will submit the draft written investigation report to the RIO 
within 40 working days of convening the investigation panel. In the event of unavoidable 
delays in gathering testimony and/or other evidence the duration of the panel’s work can be 
extended by the RIO on the request of the panel. Any delays to this timescale will be explained 
to the Complainant and Respondent in writing, presenting an estimated revised date of 
completion. 
6.3.16 The RIO may ask the Investigation Panel to clarify any of its findings and the draft report 
can be amended once agreed with the Panel.  
6.3.17 The final report must include the following:  
i. 
describe the policies and procedures under which the investigation was 
conducted;  
ii. 
describe how and from whom information relevant to the investigation was 
obtained; 
iii. 
 state whether the allegations have been upheld in full, upheld in part, or not 
upheld, and indicate the level of seriousness of any misconduct, give the reasons

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 7  
 
for and context of the findings, and explain the basis for the findings and record 
any differing views between members of the Investigation Panel;  
iv. 
address any procedural matters that the investigation has brought to light within 
SETU and/or relevant partner organisations; and  
v. 
Where an allegation of research misconduct has been upheld, in full or in part, 
make a recommendation on whether the research record needs to be corrected 
and whether the matter should be referred to the appropriate disciplinary 
procedure. The final report will include all comments received from the 
complainant, or an accurate summary of those comments, and the response of the 
Investigation Panel to those comments.  
6.3.18 The RIO will provide the Respondent with a copy of the draft investigation report for 
comment and rebuttal. The Respondent will be allowed to review and comment within 15 
working days of receipt of the draft report. Comments should be on matters of fact only. The 
Respondent’s comments will be attached to the final report. The findings of the final report 
should take into account the Respondent’s comments in addition to all the other evidence.  
6.3.19 The RIO will provide the Complainant, if they are identifiable, with those portions of 
the draft investigation report that address the Complainant's role and opinions in the 
investigation. The report should be modified, as appropriate, based on the Complainant's 
comments. Comments should be on matters of fact only.  
6.3.20 The complainant and respondent must treat the final report confidentially. 
6.3.21 The Investigation Panel Chair will forward the final report with the complainant’s and 
respondent’s comments attached to the RIO. 
7. 
Institutional Review and Decision   
 
7.1 The RIO along with other institutional officials, to include the HR manager where the 
Respondent is a staff member or the Vice President Academic Affairs, Teaching & Learning 
where the Respondent is a postgraduate research student, the relevant Head of Faculty, and 
the Vice President for Research Innovation and Impact will consider the investigation report 
and decide what additional steps or actions, if any, should be taken.  
7.2 The Institutional Review, and the Decision of the Institutional Review, should be 
completed within 20 working days of the RIO receiving the final report of the Investigation 
Panel. 
7.3 The RIO may return the report to the Investigation Panel with a request for further fact-
finding or analysis.  
7.4 SETU will take appropriate administrative actions when an allegation of misconduct has 
been substantiated. These administrative actions may include referral to another policy or 
referral to the appropriate disciplinary procedure. In the case of staff, the Institute of 
Technology Disciplinary Procedure would apply, and in the case of postgraduate research

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 8  
 
students, the SETU Student Academic Misconduct Policy and Disciplinary Procedure would 
apply. 
7.5 If the matter is to be referred to the appropriate SETU disciplinary procedure, all 
evidence, information and reports shall be provided to the appropriate line manager and a 
copy to the  HR manager, in the case of a Respondent who is an employee, or the Vice 
President Academic Affairs, Teaching & Learning in the case of a Respondent who is a 
postgraduate research student. Please note when processing personal data the GDPR must 
be adhered to. 
7.6 After the Investigation Report has been subject to the Institutional Review and Decision 
step, and additional steps (collectively “Institutional Decision”), if any, have been agreed, the 
RIO will notify both the Respondent and the Complainant in writing of the Institutional 
Decision. The RIO will also notify the Respondent of the right to appeal the outcome of the 
Institutional Decision (See Section 8 below).  
7.7 The RIO will determine as part of the Institutional Decision whether external agencies such 
as the Gardaí, professional societies, regulators, editors of journals in which falsified reports 
may have been published, collaborators of the Respondent in the research, external funding 
agencies or other relevant parties should be notified of the outcome of the case.  
7.8 Following completion of any disciplinary procedure as a result of a finding of research 
misconduct, the RIO should be notified if the disciplinary procedure has been concluded. 
 
8. Appeal Stage  
 
8.1 The Respondent may, within 10 working days of receipt of the Institutional Decision, make 
an appeal in relation to the outcome of the Institutional Decision, and clearly set out the 
grounds of the appeal in writing to the RIO accompanied by supporting documentation.  
8.2 Such appeal should be limited to the following grounds: 
o Procedural irregularities: There has been material procedural 
irregularities in terms of fair process and adherence to procedure in the 
conduct of the investigation that could have had a material impact on 
the Institutional Decision. 
o New material information: There is new information relevant to the 
investigation which could have materially impacted the Institutional 
Decision which was not (for valid and expressly referenced reasons) 
available at the time of investigation and is now available.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  1 9  
 
8.3 The complainant should be informed of the application for appeal and on what grounds 
this application has been made within 10 working days of the written appeal being received. 
8.4 A Review Officer should be appointed by the Vice President for Research Innovation and 
Impact within 10 working days of receipt of the written appeal to undertake a review in 
accordance with the specific grounds raised by the Respondent. The Review Officer: 
• May be internal or external to SETU.  
• Must not have been involved in any way in any earlier stage of the complaints process.  
• Must declare any conflict of interest prior to appointment.  
• Must maintain confidentiality throughout the appeal process. 
8.5 The Review Officer will review the appeal request and the grounds that have been 
submitted, rather than carry out a re-investigation of the allegation(s) in question. The Review 
Officer may request additional documentation or clarification from the Respondent, 
Complainant, or Investigation Panel needed. 
8.6  Following the review, the Review Officer may determine one or more of the following 
outcomes: 
(a) Uphold the Institutional Decision and dismiss the appeal; 
(b) Uphold the appeal of the Institutional Decision in whole or in part with Institutional 
Decision being amended to meet specified conditions; 
(c) Uphold the appeal of the Institutional Decision and direct that a new investigation panel 
be appointed to conduct a new investigation (in whole or in part); 
(d) Make general recommendations in relation to the process, to the Research Integrity 
Officer.  
8.7 The findings of the Review Officer shall be communicated by the Review Officer to those 
parties who have been previously informed of the Institutional Decision, with a copy to the 
Research Integrity Officer. 
8.8 Minor deviations from the prescribed procedures or timelines will not normally constitute 
grounds for appeal unless they can be shown to have caused substantial prejudice to the 
Respondent or otherwise materially affected the fairness of the process. 
8.9 The decision of the Review Officer shall be final. The RIO shall ensure that all necessary 
actions arising from the findings of the Review Officer are implemented.8.10 The review 
should normally be completed within 30 working days of the Review Officer’s appointment, 
unless exceptional circumstances apply.

Research Misconduct Procedures 
Version 1.0 
| P a g e  2 0  
 
9. Data Retention 
 
9.1 The RIO shall, in a secure and access limited location on the university system, maintain 
confidential records of all stages of proceedings under this procedure. The RIO will retain all 
reports, correspondence, transcripts of meetings and other documentation relating to the 
operation of this Procedure. 
9.2 The Inquiry Panel and Investigation Panel Chairs shall keep accurate records of the 
activities, deliberation and reporting of their respective panels and provide these records to 
the RIO for inclusion in the archive of the case upon the completion of their work. 
9.3 The RIO shall maintain all records on file for reporting and auditing purposes - in order to 
meet obligations to the research funder(s) and any collaborator(s). Such records may include 
the personal data of researchers. The university has a legitimate interest and contractual 
obligations to its funders to retain such data.  
9.4 Data will be retained in accordance with SETU Data Inventory Retention Schedule 
document for retention periods www.setu.ie/dataprotection. 
 
9.5 Upon the conclusion of an investigation, at whatever stage, the RIO is responsible for the 
accurate, timely and confidential transfer of information to any relevant parties, including HR 
for any disciplinary procedure.  
9.6 Reports generated by an investigation under this Procedure may be used in evidence by 
subsequent investigations under this Procedure, where a related matter is raised, or by 
other Organisational processes (such as a disciplinary process).  
 
9.7 
All documents will be subject to Freedom of Information and Subject Access 
Requests.

12. 
Responsibility and Authority 
The Vice President of Research, Innovation and Impact is responsible for the implementation 
and monitoring of compliance with these procedures. 
13. 
Compliance 
 
These procedures are applicable to all individuals and groups who carry out or support 
research activities under the auspices of SETU. These procedures apply to all permanent, part-
time or contract staff employed by SETU or any individuals participating in research on any 
SETU campuses or on behalf of SETU at any location. 
 
14. Policy Author 
 
The author of these procedures is the Research Integrity and Compliance Officer. Any 
feedback or issues arising on implementation of these procedures should be communicated 
to the RIO.  
 
15. 
Review of Procedures 
 
These procedures will be reviewed at the end of the first year of operation, and thereafter, 
every three years.
